Black is in check from the bishop on d5.

1. Move the King.
   - g8h8: Legal (moves king out of the line of attack).
   - g8f8: Not legal (rook is there).
   - g8h7: Not legal (pawn is there).
   - g8f7: Not legal (square is empty but the bishop still attacks it). Wait, if the king moves to f7, he is still on the diagonal d5-h1.
2. Block the check.
   - f8f7: Legal (the rook moves to f7, blocking the diagonal d5-g8).

So the options are g8h8 (move the king) or f8f7 (block the check).
Now, which one is better?
If I play f8f7, I block the check, but the bishop on d5 can capture the rook: d5xf7.
If I play g8h8, I move the king out of check and keep the rook on f8.
g8h8 seems like the much better move, as it preserves the rook.

g8: (7, 8)
Difference in files: 7 - 4 = 3
Difference in ranks: 8 - 5 = 3
Since the differences are equal, it is a diagonal.
Yes, the bishop at d5 is checking the king at g8.

1. g8h8: Move king to h8. (Removes check, keeps rook)
2. f8f7: Block check with the rook. (Removes check, but rook can be captured by d5xf7)

Clearly, g8h8 is the better move.
